This is simply because the proper implementation of intensive operations is only possible under military conditions of experience. We must imagine that dozens of helicopters have been used in a narrow valley under poor visual conditions. This can only be achieved if the pilots are trained on such massive deployments, and this is usually not the case with private companies.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e3822	Mr. Federal Minister! My question is: 270/M What role does the Austrian Federal Army play in a modern crisis management?
ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e3849	Please, Mr. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e3865	The question of crisis management and the question of Austria's participation have undoubtedly experienced a special topicality through recent European decisions. In itself, the so-called Petersberg tasks can be assumed. This means that we are dealing with humanitarian operations, here we are dealing with efforts to find and save and, of course, peacekeeping and peacekeeping. The Treaty of Amsterdam now gives the European Union a competence in this field and thus also a responsibility for a certain extent of the military sector. Of course, there is also a certain obligation to prepare for it. Therefore, at their meeting in Cologne, for example, the prime ministers and Chancellors of the EU countries decided not only that it would be necessary to continue the efforts to defend them accordingly, but also to provide for logistical transport. This is a vulnerability in the European Union. This is also a reason for us to intensify the reflections on transport capacity as far as the area is concerned. This is not only because of the concrete experience we have in Albania and other operations, but also because it is part of a pan-European strategy in the long term. Therefore, we will not only take these tasks, which have been taken over by us as a new member of the EU, but also take them very seriously, in particular, in our own priorities.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e3889	Additional question? - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e3904	Federal Minister! Do the new decisions of the Cologne European Council play a special role here, and do they go in a certain direction?
ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e3920	Please, Mr. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e3935	They are heading in the direction that Europe must be able to carry out a larger part of the crisis management itself and possibly on its own. The organisation for this purpose will be the European Union, which, for the first time, will undoubtedly take a new approach with a responsible person for a common foreign and security policy, but also for the first time with the establishment of a military staff. However, it must be assumed – and this is also stated in the relevant documents – that the European Union will for a long time not yet be able to deal with major crises alone, and therefore a very close cooperation with NATO will be necessary. What has also been considered is simply not just a transfer, but even a merger of the tasks of the Western European Union, and hence of the European defence alliance, into the European Union. This is, however, a process in which the discussion is not yet completed, but in which the appropriate adjustments are to be made by the end of next year. In my view, what we can say about this is that it will certainly be necessary to deal with this question during this period, namely by the end of next year, if we do not want the question of European security to be dealt with without us, that it should be done without regard to our interests, that it should be carried out without the possibility of bringing in our own ideas there, so that one day we would have to adopt a model that others had designed without even having the possibility of participation. This is actually the reason why we will probably have to deal very intensively with this question next year.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e4033	Mr Mag. Barmüller, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e4048	Federal Minister! In particular, with regard to the Amsterdam Treaty, they have said that there will also be peace-making measures in which Austria will be able to participate in the future. This, of course, creates tensions with our status of neutrality. My question, therefore, is: In what areas and in what specific measures – crossings and crossings, for example – do you see tensions with neutrality in the coming years when such a model is developed, as you have now mentioned?
ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e4066	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e4082	There is no doubt that after the end of the Cold War, a change in security needs and the security situation as a whole has occurred, which can essentially be characterized by the fact that no longer the great war between two blocks is in the foreground, but undoubtedly the handling of local and regional crises using the state community. There is no doubt that, under the command of solidarity, everyone is making a possible contribution. How large and how strong this solidarity is, in each individual case, is decided by each country. This is also a misunderstanding, which is very common in Austria, that, for example, as a NATO member, one would be forced to take part in an action automatically with a certain military quota. That's not right. In each individual case, each country itself determines the measure and the scope of its action. It is quite true, for example, that only part of the NATO countries have participated in the current use of Kosovo. Some have not participated, even as the example of Greece has shown, is very distant. In other words, this automatic commitment does not exist for crisis operations. What we can say, however, is that what is expected of the Community of States is, without a doubt, a solidarity - at least solidarity that does not hinder others. I could say, for example, that, as far as Kosovo's use is concerned, the use of war could now be brought to an end thanks to the fact that the Yugoslav government was willing to withdraw its troops, police forces and militias and thus make it possible to return the refugees again, even in the countries which did not go into such a commitment with overwhelming enthusiasm – if I am thinking of the Dutch or Belgian armed forces – it is already unacceptable for a state which is in the same political community, with which there is even the same security policy orientation, because there is a common foreign foreign and security policy, or if it is to be intensified in the future, for soldiers who are to be used by a democratic country in the interests of the State for the purpose of carrying out humanitarian purposes, not to receive adequate support at least in the form that it is not an external country, as was the case here, such as Slovakia, which is not a member of the EU, who must seek overflight, if they need to return from the territory or use it. [[Abg. Dr. Schmidt: That's not the question!]] This is something that has also been discussed very intensively in the Dutch Parliament. This should undoubtedly also be an approach for us to reflect on this new form of solidarity, because it has become apparent that some of our own political partners in the political community have absolute misunderstandings that we cannot impose these solidarity benefits.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e4122	The next supplementary question was raised by Mr Antoni. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e4137	Federal Minister! Modern crisis management also means being able to provide rapid assistance or to implement rapid measures. How much do you appreciate the time period that Austria needs to provide assistance on the ground, or are there measures to keep the period as short as possible?
ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e4155	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e4171	Of course, it always comes in the way of the accident events. We are able to provide effective assistance within a few hours in the field of search and rescue, and have repeatedly demonstrated this, also in the international field. We were able, for example, when the fire disaster on the Croatian coast occurred, within 24 hours of the time when the accident was known not only to meet all the formal requirements, including the approvals by the Croatian government et cetera, but also to create all the conditions so that our soldiers and helicopter pilots were actually able to go in this operation within a few hours. In the case of larger operations, such as the forthcoming Kosovo deployment, a peace deployment, it is of course necessary to prepare longer periods of time. It is assumed at international level that a period of about six weeks to three months is in any case necessary in order to prepare the soldiers for these very concrete missions. For this is not only about making them aware of their military tasks, but also about familiarizing them with the circumstances in the country accordingly. This, of course, is also about preparing appropriate material. There are thousands of tons of material going down there. All of this must, if one wants to meet the safety requirements, be guaranteed in advance. It is assumed that this will include a preparation period of six to twelve weeks.

The second criticism of the current behaviour of banks is: If I collect money from the account, the amount is due the same day, but if I pay on the account money, the amount will only be put on the account the next day currencyically. In other words, one day lies in between, making the banks a huge profit in interest. No bank is preoccupied in this area by acting in a friendly manner. ParlaMint-AT_1999-06-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00175_d7e13357	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Three brief comments on the Transfer Act; the first concerns the legal or technical nature of this Act. We have been led to this transfer law by the implementation of the transparency directive of the European Union. Interesting in this implementation is the fact that it was originally intended to immediately implement this matter by means of a government template. As a result, an interesting constitutional discussion has taken place as to whether this direct and direct applicability of the directives required under the EU Treaty is moving. The nature of a directive is that, although it is binding on its objective, the individual national states can choose how they implement it. When they are implemented almost identically word-identically, the question arises whether the immediate applicability is an exploitation of the actual free space or whether that contradicts the permitted perception of the free space. In this respect, there has been an interesting constitutional discussion with the Constitutional Service of the Federal Chancellery. This has now been resolved by federal law, because of the relatively comprehensive amendment which, however, does not differ from what was already in place in the directive. The second point is the content of what Mr Peter has already mentioned. Of course, it would have been preferable for the Chamber of Workers and Consumer Protection to extend the scope to domestic transfers even if higher compensation had been provided for if there had been own dispute resolution procedures and further customer protection provisions had been introduced. But, unfortunately, it was not possible to agree, above all, with the colleagues from the Chamber of Commerce, on these further things; therefore, a further proposal was not available politically. In my opinion, however, this issue remains on the agenda for further discussions. It was in the last sentence of § 4 para. 1 once mistakenly used the term "contractor" instead of properly "contractor". And that is a fundamental difference. It is common practice in Austria that interest rates for minor transfers, Mr. Federal Minister, are charged in the order of 11, 12 and 13 percent. The EU Directive does not say that there must be, in other words, that this will be excluded in foreign payment transactions, but it is still possible in domestic payment transactions. The fact that there is a directive which states that it is necessary to regulate it in cross-border transport should not have been allowed to prepare a corresponding scheme, i.e. to put the whole into a law which also applies to domestics. Therefore, we will not give our consent to this proposal – not for a second reason, because we believe that there is a serious defect in the Finality Act. The directive also requires that the credit instrument, whether directly or indirectly, be organised in a clearing and settlement system or in several. In fact, the law becomes much more restrictive and requires very concrete standardisation. The Federal Ministry of Justice is quite right to say that this regulation is above the target, because there is also an indirect participation in a clearing and settlement system, and one should not have gone that far. In this respect, too much intervention is being made in the market, but on the other hand, important things are left open. These are all reasons why we cannot agree with the best knowledge and conscience of this template.

We are opposed to extending access rights to rental contracts, because this greatly restricts property rights. Any legal extension of a rental contract to persons other than the tenant means, of course, at the same time a restriction for the owner of the property, who can no longer choose his tenants. Any extension is therefore also an attack on the freedom of contract. From these considerations, we have always rejected these wishes to extend access rights to persons other than those currently mentioned in the law. For if someone wants to have his home if someone wants to choose the people who are to be in this apartment, in this case there is the Institute of Housing Property. After all, the deficit has succeeded in reducing the deficit from 5 percent of GDP to 3.7 percent, then 2.7 percent and finally 2.5 percent. Ladies and gentlemen! But in practical policy I sometimes feel that this idea is extremely modest intergrouply, so it is very difficult when problems arise, even from the expenditure side, to ask for certain measures, because, of course, it is not easy to finally avoid budget sums which are in the proposal and on which certain concepts and considerations have been set out by the ministers of the department. I therefore believe that it is important – and I stress this because the Federal Government has taken such a decision with regard to the future budget-making – that we should be well advised in the framework of the budget programmes for the next few years if we are to pursue a significant expenditure policy and focus on rowing nominal growth on the one hand and on expenditure development on the other in a certain percentage ratio. This is very important when one sees the objectives that have been expressed on several occasions, namely that one or more concerns about the Stability Pact and compliance with the objectives. I am very happy if this is not only noted, but also if it is taken into account that practical implementation in the policy of this stability rate and the Stability Pact can also be achieved. I would now like to come up with a number of detailed questions which have been mentioned in the context of the debate, as regards which it seems necessary to me to make a few comments. The pure transport costs, which on average only amount to about 1 to a maximum of 10 percent of the total price of a product, will increase by a maximum of between 3 and 11 percent. In any event, the toll will also have an extremely low impact on the consumer price index. The Wifo has identified an increase of around 0.1 to a maximum of 0.2 percent for the consumer price index. High house! There is also a clarification: Road pricing means that those who produce "just in place" have a significant advantage over those who have to deliver "just in time". Single: The necessary funds for road construction must be increased in a way that does not confuse the budget plan and that allows Austria to continue to comply with the Maastricht criteria. The Federal Road Finance Act is a real step in this direction. Ladies and gentlemen! The financing of the Austrian road construction projects must also be taken into account in announcements as to how the German Chancellor's climate in the glowing Tauernröhre has still been pronounced. With these amendments to the Federal Road Finance Act and the ASFINAG Authorization Act, several effects can be achieved. First: On a precisely defined federal road network, namely on the Bundesstraßes A and the Bundesstraßes S – i.e. motorways and highways – on the B 301, the B 302 and the B 315, a driving power dependent toll for motor vehicles of more than three and a half tons of maximum permissible weight will be lifted. For economic and technical reasons, it may be necessary to include, or also exempt from, certain federal roads or federal roads in the toll system quickly and flexibly. This too is flexibly regulated by this law, because the following must be the priority: safety, speed and liquidity of transport. Secondly, As a result of its driving performance-dependent design, ASFINAG will be available for road construction every year for more than 2.3 billion schilling. This will enable the further expansion of the high-level road network transferred to it to take place gradually, without, and I would like to stress, the need for annual budget allocations at this level. This law also leads to a budget relief, which can only be in the sense of all of our Austrian citizens. Thirdly, We believe that the start and introduction of road pricing in Austria can only take place in the same way as our main trading and transport partner, that is, the Federal Republic of Germany. [[Abg. Blünegger: That's what the Chamber of Commerce says, yes!]] According to the report, this will be in 2002/2003. Therefore, and also for reasons of the time needed for the establishment of the necessary infrastructure such as toll stations and so on, the law sets out 2002 and at the same time provides for the possibility to change this by regulation. We do not want to afford a single European approach. Fourth: The system is currently a semi-open system. However, we will endeavour – and again there is the possibility of regulating this by regulation – to use the technical possibilities to make use of the ongoing improvements to be observed annually, so that we can install the best system that will be available at the time of introduction. That too is guaranteed by this law. Fifth, you open a bottle Ladies and gentlemen! In the sense of all road users, in the sense of road safety and ultimately also in the sense of budget relief, this law is necessary and correct. It therefore has my approval and, above all, the approval of my group.

The question is: Who is responsible for this success? – I mean, there's a symbiosis: On the one hand, it is of course those entrepreneurs who have recognized the structural change and used the opportunity of structural change, on the other hand, the employees in this industry are also very skilled; and finally, we also find a changed scene in the area of marketing and advertising. I believe that Austria's advertising has its share of this success. One of the most important reasons is that the industry has succeeded in truly planting the idea of cooperation, and that not only the cooperation of enterprises among themselves, but also the tourism industry with other sectors is taking place. For example, one might mention Bad Harbach in the forest district, where tourism cooperates very closely with agriculture. In addition, tourism organisations, the regions, are starting to work together very slowly in marketing. I believe that this is also due to the fact that the Socialists once tabled a motion for a resolution - after the long opposition of Mr Puttinger we decided - when the regional tourism organisations saw the light of the world, so that they can now create regional brands and thus shape the regions accordingly. [[Abg. Eder: Exactly!]] Ladies and gentlemen! This rethinking, which has taken place, must now, in order to ensure the higher qualities in the future, also take place in the area of workers. I can only appeal to the economy to recognize that the high quality of tourism can only be ensured if the workplace situation is right when the environment is right. I therefore call on you to ensure equal opportunities and equality, for example by ensuring that seasonal workers are paid off in tourism. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] [[Abg. Fischl: This is a dangerous threat! A real dangerous threat!]] Ladies and gentlemen! Since my clubman gave me only 3 minutes of speaking time, I conclude: I am convinced that we should continue this path; more cooperation, more quality by creating opportunities for workers in tourism. In this sense I wish tourism all the best!
